# Build plan — Claude-Cursor Bridge, Release B

The design is the Restructuring Plan's release B (§16) and the sections it rests on (§6, §15). This plan decomposes that scope into increments. Per §15 condition 3, **governance text is design: the supervisor authors it, gated by the two-critic design review; programs are implementation: the builder writes them, gated by the delegation loop.** Each increment marks its author. Status is derived from `origin/main` by `plan-check.py`, never written here. Revised 2026-10-11 after two-critic rounds 1–3 (converging: Blocking 7→6→1 and 5→1→2). Revised 2026-10-20 at the owner's decision on the window flood of 2026-10-19/20 (ISS-011; R-039 to R-041): TASK-028 to TASK-031 added to stage B5 before TASK-025, the fix made in the bridge and installed by calibration rather than per project, as a planned update and not a hotfix (INVENTORY, Decisions); critiqued as the subject `windowless` (docs/DESIGN_CRITIQUE.md). Revised 2026-10-24 at the owner's placement of the pace update (2026-10-22, "a"; the maintainer's request of 2026-10-20 relaying the owner's three approved solutions; R-042 to R-044): TASK-032 to TASK-035 added to stage B5 beside TASK-028 to TASK-031 (TASK-035 split from TASK-034 at the critique's round 1), TASK-030's mutation text moved to one run per increment, the leads of stage B3's review placed (docs/HARDENING.md, Stage B3, "Placed by the pace plan revision") and the Scopes that review found short widened; critiqued as the subject `pace` (docs/DESIGN_CRITIQUE.md: four rounds and the confirming read) and approved at the owner's gate on 2026-10-24 with the ceiling at three rounds ("I choose three"). Revised 2026-10-24, before stage B4's TASK-017, at the supervisor's reality check of the text as built (the delegation loop's step 2 rule applied to its own text; run/supervisor/task017-reality-check.md, quoted in the critique's file): TASK-017's Scope widened to every file that holds or pins a passage it moves (project-end.md, the plan critic, the registry, seven test files) and narrowed of reflection.md and interface.md, its Done when rewritten passage by passage with the extension point each goes to, two §6.4 refinements of the registry made in the same change as planning.md (`planning.stages` widened to increments, `planning.acceptance-criteria` new), the passages no point places kept in the main workflow, the deployed-layout and bounded-remote-wait checks placed as external services' new text, the `Installer verification` parameter kept among the five, and TASK-018 to TASK-021 re-read for the same class of problem (TASK-018 depends on TASK-017 for the pointer it makes; TASK-019 and TASK-021 name their delivery kinds by key and add the kind's procedure at `project-end.delivery`); kept as one increment, since the registry refinement is made with planning.md, which every trait's planning move edits too, and each increment costs a gate pass (INVENTORY, Decisions); critiqued as the subject `traits` (docs/DESIGN_CRITIQUE.md: four rounds, the confirming read and a resolution round) and approved at the owner's gate on 2026-10-24 ("I approve of the plan"). Revised 2026-10-27 at stage B5's plan revision point (after TASK-031's merge, before TASK-030), at the owner's word of 2026-10-26 to integrate the four text items the maintainer's evaluation of 2026-10-25 gave release B (decision D4: T14, T23, FB-036, FB-040; INVENTORY, Decisions), each into the increment whose Scope already holds the text it amends, no increment added and two requirement rows extended (R-042 with T14's sentences, R-043 with the estimate): T14 (the class is the defect, not the operator that exposed it; below the ceiling a stage round ending with no REJECT but with findings the convergence rule makes blocking puts them into one fix increment through its own gate, no further stage round, and at and above it such a finding outside the five goes to HARDENING and opens no fix increment) into TASK-034's Done when; T23 (the change cycle's item 1 ends with a confirmation inside intake, not a gate) into TASK-035, its Scope widened to change-cycle.md and tests/test_phase_modules_close.py, whose pin on the procedure's bytes it re-takes, and, for FB-036, to bridge/cursor-bridge/Glossary.md and tests/test_workflow_model.py; FB-040 (the process count of a mutation run estimated before the increment's first run and any `--all` run, the duplicates the mutants file shows cut before the launch) into TASK-030's Done when and as a clause of R-043; FB-036 (the core read in parts to its end, the current phase's modules named) satisfied at calibration by TASK-023 (PR #85), the four "read whole" statements (the core's two, rule 28's and the Glossary's) made to say in parts by TASK-035 and TASK-026's "one read" clause reworded, no bound for the core taken; critiqued as the subject `maintainer-text` (docs/DESIGN_CRITIQUE.md): four rounds and a confirming read, nothing Blocking left open, no resolution round due; approved by the owner at the gate on 2026-10-27 ("Approve"). Revised 2026-11-01 for release T, the project's first change cycle (change-cycle.md, item 4; docs/DESIGN.md, "Change: release T", approved at its design gate on 2026-11-01): stages T1 and T2 appended below, TASK-036 to TASK-045, each increment drawn from the register's rows R-045 to R-056 and R-042's revision, the author marked as release B marked it (governance text and tests the supervisor's; the one program fix, BUG-039 as TASK-043, the builder's); critiqued as the subject `release-t-plan` (docs/DESIGN_CRITIQUE.md). Revised 2026-11-04 for release B2, the second change cycle (change-cycle.md, item 4; docs/DESIGN.md, "Change: release B2"): stages H1 and H2 appended below, TASK-046 to TASK-054, each drawn from the register's rows R-057 to R-064, the programs the builder's and the texts, the migration of this repository's records and the stamp the supervisor's; the addendum stood in the design's round-1 file, so it is criticised and gated with the design as one subject, `release-b2` (docs/DESIGN_CRITIQUE.md). Revised 2026-11-13 for the program release, the third change cycle (change-cycle.md, item 4; docs/DESIGN.md, "Change: the program release"): stages P1 to P4 appended below, TASK-055 to TASK-083, each drawn from the register's rows R-066 to R-104, the programs the builder's and the tests first, the texts, the records and the stamp the supervisor's; the addendum stood in the design's round-1 file, so it is criticised and gated with the design as one subject, `program` (docs/DESIGN_CRITIQUE.md). Revised 2026-11-14 for the field finding F7, a scoped revision of the program release mid-build (docs/DESIGN.md, "Change: the one-increment merge unit"; R-105): TASK-084 added to stage P2, and TASK-082, TASK-081 and TASK-065's dependences revised for it; the revision stood in the design's round-1 file, so it is criticised and gated with the design as one subject, `program-f7` (docs/DESIGN_CRITIQUE.md).

## Standing constraints (every increment)
- **Manifest:** any increment that adds, moves, removes, **or edits** a file under `bridge/` regenerates `bridge/cursor-bridge/MANIFEST.json` (`python tools/make-manifest.py`) in the same commit (a hash change fails `bridge-check --sources` in CI otherwise); `MANIFEST.json` is named in its Scope.
- **Tests name the requirement:** each increment's test contains the literal `R-nnn` of every requirement it satisfies. A governance-text increment's test is structural (the file exists at its §6.11 path, opens with its compaction-survival list where applicable, declares its extension points by the registry's names, is within the R-032 bound, and — **from TASK-006 on** — `workflow-check.py` exits 0 on it).
- **Moves, not copies:** text moved in the split (including a trait's gathered passages, §6.8) *moves* — it leaves its old home; the main workflow shrinks. A passage may move twice (baseline → phase module → trait file); `trace-check` accepts it found verbatim anywhere under `workflow/**`.
- **Trace exceptions:** `bridge/cursor-bridge/workflow/trace-exceptions.md` carries two entry kinds — `reword <TASK> <file> <heading>` and `new <TASK> <file> <heading>` — appended by the supervisor-text increment that rewords or adds; it is named in that increment's Scope so the design review sees it.
- **Open list currency:** an increment that writes a type, sub-type, or trait flips that entry's written flag in `bridge/cursor-bridge/workflow/open-list.md` (named in its Scope).
- **No hard line wraps** in any authored Markdown (rule 30).

## Prior-project lessons
The overlapping prior project is the bridge itself (`~/.claude/cursor-bridge/Known-Pitfalls.md`, the three Issues files).
**Extracted (attached to a `Done when:`).** KP-035 → TASK-010 (digest within limit), TASK-011–014 (modules read-whole), TASK-015 (one module + conditional files). Digest limit → TASK-010 keeps `test_session_start.py` green. §6.5/R11 → TASK-012 and TASK-017 (the mockup variant is an *addition*; its text lives in `interface/mockups.md`). KP-029 → TASK-006 (workflow-check fails an unwritten-type line), TASK-004 (the written column).
**Judged irrelevant.** Asset-loop/Roblox/aggregate pitfalls (E/F); code-map pitfalls (C/D).

## Stage B5 — Calibration and acceptance
Demonstration: a fresh session walked intake→configuration reads only its core, its current phase module, and its own conditional workflows; the four projects each classify under §6.3 on fixtures with `workflow-check` green; a mutation run and a test suite started together through the launcher run one after the other, the second saying what it waits for, and no window appears; the mutation run repeated with nothing changed runs no mutant, and with `--all` runs every one; a builder launch of a fourth-round brief that names no blocking class is refused by the launcher and one that names it runs, and `review-guard.py snapshot` refuses a fourth round of one increment — a snapshot after three reviewed ones — without a class line the same way and opens it with one; `pace-report.py` over the bridge's own history prints stage B5's increments with their rounds and the ones over the ceiling.

### TASK-029 — One heavy job at a time: the launcher's machine-wide slot
Author: builder.
Parent: none
Depends on: TASK-028
Parallel: no
Diagrams: none
Scope: bridge/cursor-bridge/bridge-run.py; bridge/cursor-bridge/loop-guard.py; bridge/cursor-bridge/MANIFEST.json; tests/test_bridge_run_slot.py; tests/test_loop_guard.py; docs/cli-reference.json; docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md
Satisfies: R-040
Done when: (a) a run of kind `builder`, `test` or `driver` takes one machine-wide slot before its command starts: an exclusive operating-system lock (`msvcrt.locking` on Windows, `fcntl.flock` elsewhere) on one byte of `~/.cursor-bridge/heavy.lock` — the bridge's state folder in the owner's home, where the agent home already lives (KP-038), resolved from `USERPROFILE`/`HOME` and never from `LOCALAPPDATA` or `APPDATA`, which the packaged desktop app virtualises (KP-055), outside the installed tree `bridge-check.py` walks, one for the computer and never under a project; the holder's record — its kind, run id, pid, process creation time, its `slot taken` time as `start`, and its limit — is published after the lock is taken to the sibling `heavy.lock.holder`, written to a temporary file in the same folder and moved into place with `os.replace` (KP-047: a waiter polls it), where every other process can read it while the lock is held (a byte-range lock on Windows denies a read of the locked bytes); a record counts only while a process with its pid and creation time is alive, so a dead holder's record reads as none; the lock is the truth; the lock is held for the run's life and released by the operating system when the launcher ends, however it ends, so there is no stale lock and no takeover; a `review` or `watch` run takes none; (b) a second heavy run prints `bridge-run: started <time>Z` first, as every run does, then once a minute `bridge-run: waiting for the slot held by <kind> <run id> since <start>Z, ends by <end>Z` — the end being the holder's `start` plus its limit plus a grace of 120 s — then `bridge-run: slot taken <time>Z` when its command starts; the wait is not counted against the run's ceiling, which starts at `slot taken`; its bound is fixed once, from the first live record the waiter reads — that record's end, never less than the waiter's own kind's default from the wait's start and never more than the provisional bound below — and is not recomputed when another run takes the slot meanwhile, so the total wait never exceeds the builder's cap plus the grace whatever holders come and go; while no live record is there, or none that parses, the line reads `held by unknown` with no end and the provisional bound is the builder's cap plus the grace; a wait that ends unmet exits 123 with `bridge-run: slot not free - held by <kind> <run id> since <start>Z, ends by <end>Z`, naming the holder of the last live record read and its end, or `held by unknown` when no live record was read, a code distinct from the command's limit (124) and the refusals (125), and such a run is never marked healthy; the log entry in `runs.jsonl` records `waited` apart from `seconds`, which is the command's own, so `--limit auto` learns nothing from a wait; (c) `loop-guard.py` knows the `driver` kind (its kind pattern and `KIND_CEILING`) and counts a slot-taking kind's launched command for the heavy launch's one sum — the kind's cap plus the longest wait (the builder's cap, 14,400 s) plus the grace (120 s) plus the waiter's reporting minute — with its 600 s notification grace on top, the same sum step 3's `timeout` uses (TASK-030), the driver's count pinned in `tests/test_loop_guard.py`; (d) `tests/test_bridge_run_slot.py` (names R-040): two heavy runs started together run one after the other and the second's stderr shows the waiting line naming the first's kind, id, start and end, read from the holder's record by a second process while the first still holds the lock (on Windows too), then `slot taken`; a waiter that reads before the record is published prints `held by unknown` and fixes its bound from the record once published; a holder that itself waited before taking the slot gives a waiter whose bound still covers the holder's ceiling; a review takes no slot; a holder killed mid-run frees the slot at once and its record reads as none; a second holder taking the slot during a wait leaves the waiter's bound as first fixed; a wait that outlives its bound ends with exit 123 and its line, the end time on it; `seconds` excludes the wait; the lock's path comes from the home, so the tests' temporary home (`tests/conftest.py`'s `home_of_the_tests`) isolates them from the owner's lock; `tests/test_loop_guard.py` gains the driver kind and the widened count; (e) at the inspect step the supervisor observes one launcher run waiting in the slot for more than thirty minutes, started from the Bash tool with the `timeout` step 3 names, and records under ISS-011, beside KP-053's open question, whether the tool let it reach `slot taken` — the sign of an outside kill being an `.err` holding waiting lines and neither `slot taken` nor `bridge-run: exit`; the command reference regenerated; `windowless-check.py` green; no new dependency.

### TASK-031 — Mutation reruns that skip what is unchanged: `mutation-run.py`
Author: builder.
Parent: none
Depends on: TASK-028
Parallel: no
Diagrams: none
Scope: bridge/cursor-bridge/mutation-run.py; bridge/cursor-bridge/MANIFEST.json; tests/test_mutation_run.py; docs/cli-reference.json; docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md; tests/test_dump_cli.py (widened 2026-10-23, at stage B3's review: the new program's build_parser() joins that test's list of argparse programs, which the generated command reference reads, as TASK-016's widening notes)
Satisfies: R-041
Done when: (a) `python ~/.claude/cursor-bridge/mutation-run.py <mutants file> [--all] [--checkpoint <commit>] [--scratch <folder>]` reads a JSON file the supervisor writes (`run/supervisor/<id>-mutants.json`): per mutant an id, the file, a list of `old`/`new` text pairs applied together, the test files to run, the test ids expected to fail, and an optional `expect: survive <reason>` for an equivalent mutant; one list of the increment's new tests for `--checkpoint` — test ids, or test files the checkpoint commit does not hold (a listed file the commit holds is refused, exit 2, with the message to list its new tests by id); and an optional list of untracked paths to copy beside the tracked tree (fixture data; each inside the Workspace, not a link, never entering the key); a path with `..`, an absolute path or an untracked file among the mutated or test files is refused (exit 2), so the increment's new test files are staged before the first run (`git ls-files` reads the index); (b) it removes `<scratch>/mutation-run-<id>/` where a killed run left one, then copies the project's tracked tree whole (`git ls-files`; `.venv`, `node_modules` and data are untracked and stay out), the root `.env` where present (the local service passwords the tests need, KP-032) and the listed untracked paths into that subfolder of the scratch folder (`--scratch` defaults to `~/.cursor-bridge/scratch/<project>/`, `<project>` being the project root's folder name, the Workspace's parent, since every checkout is named `Workspace`; a scratch folder inside the Workspace, containing it, or reached through a link is refused, exit 2), creates and removes only that subfolder, applies one mutant at a time there to the file's original bytes and writes those bytes back before the next, matching an `old` text with the file's own newlines (the working copies here are CRLF), runs its tests there from the scratch root with `PYTHONPATH` set to that root — so the copy precedes an editable install's `.pth` entry on `sys.path` (KP-054) — in a process started with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`, and writes nothing to the Workspace but its cache under `run/supervisor/mutation-cache/` (gitignored), so the tracked tree is byte-identical after a run and an interrupted run leaves nothing to restore (KP-056); before any test runs, in `--checkpoint` mode too, it checks, inside a pytest invocation of the named tests (`--collect-only` with a plugin that prints where the tests' package resolves, so a `src` layout or a `conftest.py` path insert resolves as the tests will), that the package resolves inside the scratch tree, and refuses with exit 2, caching nothing, when it does not; (c) outcomes: `killed` when at least one of the mutant's expected tests is FAILED in the run's `-rA` report, for whatever reason, `survived` when none is, `not applied` when an old text is not found or found twice, `error` when the tests did not collect, the run crashed, or a test not expected to fail failed; an `expect: survive` mutant is reported apart, counts as neither killed nor survived, and when killed is reported as a wrong claim of equivalence; each outcome is written as one JSON line to `run/supervisor/mutation-cache/<id>.jsonl` and flushed before the next mutant starts, a torn last line ignored on reading, so a run ended at the launcher's limit, by `bridge-stop.py` or by a dropped session keeps every kill it earned and the relaunch continues from the cache; the key is the sha256 over the sorted relative paths and contents of the tracked files under the mutated file's own folder, under the named test folders and of the root's `conftest.py`, `pytest.ini`, `pyproject.toml` and `setup.cfg` where present, plus the mutant's pairs, its expected test ids and its `expect: survive` flag — untracked files never enter it; on a later run a mutant whose key matches a recorded killed is skipped and listed `killed (cached <time>)`, every survived, not-applied or errored mutant runs again, and `--all` runs every mutant and refreshes the cache; `--checkpoint <commit>` fills the scratch tree with that commit's whole tracked tree instead (`git archive <commit>`, run in the Workspace, which holds `.git`) and the listed new tests' files as the working tree holds them, with the root `.env` and the listed untracked paths copied as in a normal run and the resolution check run on that tree, runs the listed tests there, and lists each that passes — a new test that passes on the old code proves nothing — with exit 4 when any does, while one that fails to collect there (a module the increment added) is listed `not run`, neither a pass nor a failure, and nothing else runs or is cached in that mode; the summary lists every mutant with its outcome and whether it ran, then the counts (run, cached, killed, survived, not applied, error, expected to survive); the exit code in this precedence: 3 when any mutant errored; else 1 when any survived, did not apply, or was expected to survive and was killed; else 0; 2 for a malformed file or a refused path; (d) `tests/test_mutation_run.py` (names R-041), on a fixture package and tests built in the test: a first run kills and records; a run with nothing changed runs no mutant; a change to a mutated file reruns only that file's mutants; a change to a named test file, to a `conftest.py` on its path, to a sibling of the mutated file, to the root's `pytest.ini`, to a mutant's expected test ids or to its `expect: survive` flag reruns the mutants it keys; a survived mutant reruns every time; two mutants of one file whose old texts both match the original are each applied to the original; a CRLF fixture's mutant is killed; a test reading the root `.env` and one reading a listed untracked fixture run in the copy; a crash is `error` with exit 3, never a kill, and outranks a survived mutant's 1; a test not expected to fail that fails is `error`; an equivalent mutant is reported apart, and a killed one gives exit 1; `--all` reruns all; a run killed after N mutants keeps N lines, a torn last line is ignored, and its relaunch runs only the rest; `--checkpoint` lists a new test that passes on the old code with exit 4, lists one importing a module absent at the checkpoint as `not run`, runs a listed new test reading the root `.env`, and refuses a listed file the commit holds with exit 2; the tracked tree is byte-identical after a run; an old text not found is not applied with exit 1; a package resolving outside the scratch tree is refused with exit 2 and nothing cached, in `--checkpoint` mode too, while an editable install of the fixture package, flat or under `src/`, is overridden by the copy and runs; on Windows the test process reports no console window; a malformed file exits 2; the command reference regenerated; `windowless-check.py` green; no new dependency. (Noted 2026-10-24, at stage B3's review, round 3: `plan-check.py` derives TASK-031 as merged from 9f6ace6, BUG-018's squash, whose subject's closing parenthesis names "TASK-031's Scope" (ISS-013); TASK-031 is unbuilt until its own pull request merges, so until then the plan check's READY is not trusted for TASK-031 or for TASK-030 and TASK-025, which depend on it, and TASK-031's brief is compared with this Scope by hand, the plan check's BRIEF comparison running only for unmerged increments; the plan check's rule is TASK-033's, after which that subject merges nothing.)

## Release T — the plan addendum (2026-11-01)

Release T's increments follow the standing constraints above and three of their own, from R-056 and the design's Testing strategy: **tests first** — every changed or added sentence is pinned by a test the supervisor writes before the text, each new test shown red on the increment's checkpoint by the loop's step 4 command as it stands at the increment — `python ~/.claude/cursor-bridge/bridge-run.py --limit auto --kind driver -- python ~/.claude/cursor-bridge/mutation-run.py run/supervisor/<id>-mutants.json --checkpoint <the increment's checkpoint commit>` through TASK-041's merge, and from it the stripped form TASK-041 writes, `--limit auto --strip --kind driver` with `--scratch ~/.cursor-bridge/scratch/<the project root's folder name>` among `mutation-run.py`'s options, since a stripped driver's home is the agent home and the default scratch folder would land there, the Workspace's `.env` copied with it — (the mutants file's `new_tests` listing a test file the checkpoint does not hold by its path, and a test in a file the checkpoint holds — rewritten, or new in a file committed before the checkpoint, as TASK-043's reproduction tests are — by its node id, `tests/<file>.py::<test>`, since the program refuses a listed file the checkpoint holds, a parametrised test by its full bracketed id, `::<test>[<parameter>]`, for the new parameter alone, since the bare function id matches every parameter and the old ones pass; the run's per-test lines read, every listed test shown failed on the old text and none `not run` or skipped, exit 0 alone saying only that none passed) and the run named in the accept commit, its per-test lines quoted — TASK-044's tests excepted, which characterise behaviour release B built and are shown strong by a mutants file over the code instead, as its Done when says, TASK-041's strip test, an environment test and not a pin, which skips unstripped and passes on any tree, and those of TASK-043's negative cases that hold on the unfixed guard too, invariants shown strong by a hand mutation of the fixed guard instead; **the register's Evidence names the test per item label** of a bundled row, in one grammar: a label is the leading token of a parenthesis in the row's Requirement cell when it is a letter D, T or P with digits followed by `)`, `,`, `:` or `;`, with every further such token the parenthesis lists after commas before any prose — `(P5: step 8 and ...)` gives P5, `(T24, P6)` both, `(P2, the by-class line of 2026-10-27)` P2, and `(T18's rule)` none — and an Evidence cell lists `<label>: <tests/file.py or node id>` entries separated by `;`, a row with no label naming at least one test; the increment whose accept satisfies a row's last item flips the row to built and writes its Evidence in its records-only commit (T10; `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` and `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` are outside every Scope by `scope-check.py`'s RECORDS rule, which exempts a record changed in records-only commits alone — a commit mixing a record with code is the builder's and its record edit drift — so a record an accept commit must carry is named in Scope, as TASK-045's are), a row two increments satisfy staying planned until the later one (R-052 and R-055 both flipped at TASK-044's accept, R-052 once its dispatch is read), `completion-check.py` reading the Status column as written, TASK-045 the one exception (R-056's flip rides in its accept commit, as its Done when says); and **the trace** — every reworded or added passage of the core and the modules has its `reword` or `new` entry in `trace-exceptions.md` and `trace-check` is green at every text increment's accept. A text increment's pins on sentences it rewords are re-taken in the same change (the files release B's pins live in: `tests/test_core_carve.py`, `tests/test_phase_modules_*.py`, `tests/test_b3_least_confident.py`, `tests/test_session_start.py`, `tests/test_loop_text_*.py`, `tests/test_allowlist.py`; and the older `tests/test_a5_followups.py`, which pins the stage review's part sentence and KP-061's heading, and `tests/test_leads_b3.py`, which pins design.md's snapshot-hash bullet), each named in the accept commit, the Scope naming the ones the design already shows. Every increment's pull request carries `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` as it reads at that pull request's merge (ISS-009), so the full suite runs with the pull-request variables before its first push (ISS-022; R-045's P7, the predicted `<n>`), in P7's documented form (TASK-038's (b)) from the first increment on, whose first launch, TASK-036's, is observed running with no prompt and no refusal and recorded in the design's host table as the `--key-file` word's row in TASK-036's records-only commit (`docs/DESIGN.md` a record; TASK-036's Done when states the launch and the observation, TASK-038's (c) the repair where it was prompted or refused), so that TASK-041 observes `--strip` alone. No increment runs in parallel (one heavy slot; the text increments edit shared files). The trait installable's packaging stage does not apply (the delivery kind is host-installation; the type's delivery is TASK-045's install); no increment adds or changes a command, so the command reference's currency check runs unchanged (`tools/dump-cli.py --check` in CI and the pre-commit gate). Until TASK-044 merges, `python ~/.claude/cursor-bridge/diagram-check.py --mode code` lists `TESTS:` problem lines only, beside its own `note:` lines and its `RESULT: <n> problem(s)` line — thirteen through TASK-042's checkpoint (the `Enforced by` cells of D-002, D-003 and D-004 naming no test that names the diagram's ID; D-003's ten states no test names), twelve from TASK-042's merge, whose `tests/test_dependency_scan.py` names D-004 and closes that line, the count falling as the named tests land and never rising: the loop's step 4 (Inspect, where the command stands) reads it so at every increment before TASK-044, and any `IMPORT` or `SCHEMA` line, or a count that rises, is a regression.

## Stage T1 — Release T: the text, pinned
Demonstration: on the tree as built at the stage's close: the full suite green, every test the stage's increments wrote first shown red on its increment's checkpoint by the `--checkpoint` run the accept commit names (three re-run live, their per-test lines read: TASK-036's pitfalls test, TASK-038's by-id run for the bound test's `merge.md` parameter, TASK-039's two convergence tests); `trace-check.py` green with release T's `reword` and `new` entries (nothing dropped); `tests/test_session_start.py` green (the digest within 9,500 characters, every rule number present); `python ~/.claude/cursor-bridge/diagram-check.py --mode design` OK and `--mode code` ending `RESULT: 13 problem(s)`, its problem lines the thirteen `TESTS:` lines and no other kind; `bridge-check.py --sources` naming no stale file; and `session-start.py` run over `tests/fixtures/workflows/pass/` naming the delegation loop's new sub-file `delegation-loop/merge.md` after the module, as its `Sub-files:` line gives it.
Alters: governance text only — no program's behaviour (`session-start.py`'s digest text within its cap; `settings.bridge.json`, the installer's data, gains six allow rules: TASK-038's `git branch -D` and the two `gh` list reads of P7's prediction, TASK-040's two for the stage-review tag and `git branch -r` for item 5); everything else unchanged.

## Stage T2 — Release T: the scan, the fix, the retrofit's teeth and the stamp

## Release B2 — the plan addendum (2026-11-04)

Release B2's increments follow the standing constraints above, release T's three where they apply (**tests first** for every text increment, the register's Evidence **per item label**, and **the trace** for every reworded or added passage of the core and the modules), and seven of their own, from R-057 to R-064 and the design's Testing strategy. **The programs are the builder's, their acceptance tests the supervisor's and named from the start** (ISS-017): for each program increment the supervisor writes, before the brief, the tests that pin the design's values — the schema's keys and types against D-005, the `pyyaml` cross-check, the agreement and malformed cases on fixtures the test writes — commits them before the increment's checkpoint, names them in the brief as fixed points the builder may not edit (the fix variant's rule applied to a build brief), and lists each in the mutants file's `new_tests` by node id where it is red on the checkpoint, the rest invariants shown strong by the increment's mutants; the builder's own tests join them. A supervisor's test of a name the builder adds reaches it through `getattr(bridge_env, "<name>", None)` (or the program's module loaded by file) and fails on `None` naming it, so the test collects on the checkpoint and is red there, never an error without a node id. **Every existing test stays green**: the fallback is today's code kept whole in rule, moved into `bridge_env.py` as one copy per field, so no test of a program's reading is rewritten for the header — a test that pins a retired second reading (a bold `Satisfies` line outside an increment, a forgiving `Workflows:` parse) names the design's rule that retires it and is rewritten first, red on the checkpoint; `test_r001_status_names_its_own_merge` and every test that parses the status file are re-read at TASK-054, the one increment that changes a record's shape in this repository. **R-057 flips built at TASK-050**, the later of the two increments that build its clauses (TASK-046 the reader, the schemas and the cross-check; TASK-050 the currency test over the design's enumerated retired readings), so TASK-050's Satisfies names it beside R-061 and TASK-046's merge leaves the row planned, and **R-062 flips built at TASK-052**, the later of its two increments (TASK-051 the program, TASK-052 the text), TASK-051's merge leaving it planned; a program's prose (a docstring, a comment, a help text) never quotes a retired pattern, since the currency test matches a use with docstrings and comments stripped and a pattern quoted in prose would be a hidden copy or a false match (KP-073), each program brief saying so. **The reader and the agreement checks are rule-heavy** (ISS-007, ISS-019): every program increment's mutants file (TASK-046 to TASK-051) sweeps them with a recorded operator set (a key dropped from a schema, a type widened, a refusal removed, a comparison inverted, the fallback taken where a header exists, the bound raised, a retired reading restored), the set written in the mutants file's `operators` comment and quoted in the accept commit, before the stage review reads the stage. **No record of this repository carries a header before TASK-054**, which migrates them with the installed program after the stamp and the install (the Development data decision: a session runs the installed bridge, never the one being edited, so the Workspace copy of `records-migrate.py` runs only on fixtures and scratch copies): the program increments test on fixtures and copies, so every status-file test and every pull request's P7 suite run on the shape the tests know until that increment, whose pull request runs the full suite with the pull-request variables before its first push (ISS-022) on the migrated shape. Every pull request carries `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` as it reads at that pull request's merge (ISS-009). No increment runs in parallel (one heavy slot; the program increments share `bridge_env.py` and the tests). **A plan `Scope:` line holds no semicolon inside a parenthesis**, since the fallback splits the line on `;` and the migration would write the parenthesis's halves as two paths; the addendum's lines are written so, the one reader refuses an entry with an unbalanced parenthesis (TASK-048: a shape failure for an unmerged increment; TASK-051: `cannot read <path>: unbalanced parenthesis in <id>'s Scope`, the record left header-less), so the older stages' lines, history the TDP-style `historic` mark does not cover, are listed by `records-migrate.py --check` at TASK-054 and repaired in the prose before the run, and stage H2's reflection re-reads every migrated header against its prose. The trait installable's packaging stage does not apply (the delivery kind is host-installation; the type's delivery is the install TASK-053's run sheet asks for); **the stage review of the release's text runs before the stamp** (the type's rule at `planning.stages`): stage H2's review reads the stage's whole change after TASK-052 merges and before TASK-053, the stamp, and TASK-054, which changes this repository's records alone, is reviewed by its own gate. TASK-051 adds one command, `records-migrate.py`, so it carries the command reference regenerated and committed (`tools/dump-cli.py --check` in CI and the pre-commit gate), and D-002's `dump_cli` edge to it, the edge the supervisor's commit on the increment's branch (a diagram is a record). `diagram-check.py --mode design` passes from the design's commit on; `--mode code` lists one `TESTS:` line for D-005 (its enforcing test absent) until TASK-046's merge closes it, and `check:components` passes throughout since every new import is an edge D-002 draws.

## Stage H1 — Release B2: the reader, the schemas and the programs
Demonstration: on the tree as built at the stage's close, in a scratch copy of this repository's records with headers written by hand from the design's table beside their header-less originals: each of the twelve programs (`loop-guard.py`, `session-start.py`, `conformance-check.py`, `workflow-check.py --status`, `inventory-check.py`, `evaluate-reports.py`, `plan-check.py`, `scope-check.py`, `completion-check.py`, `diagram-check.py`, and `bridge-run.py`'s and `review-guard.py`'s reads through `bridge_env`) run on the record with the header and on the one without and gives the same answer, read from each run's printed lines; one header made malformed (a tab, an unknown key, a wrong type, a bare date) refused by a checker with `HEADER MALFORMED: <path>:<line>` and exit 2, fallen back on by `session-start.py` with its one plain line, and refused by `bridge-run.py` with exit 125 and `bridge-run: refused - header: `; one header key changed against its prose line failed by its checker with `HEADER DISAGREES: <key>` and exit 1 (2 from `scope-check.py`), a brief's `satisfies`, a stage's `name`, an increment's `parallel` written `yes` in the prose and `false` in the header, and the status file's `phase` and `awaiting_user_on` (the hook's `header disagrees: <key>` line shown for each, and the loop guard's refused stop with the same line) among the keys tried, the brief with its `scope` changed refused by `bridge-run.py` with `bridge-run: refused - header: disagrees scope`, a run-parameters header whose `ceiling_above_twice` entry differs from its `Ceiling above twice:` line refused by `bridge-run.py` with `header: disagrees ceiling_above_twice` before any launch, the loop guard refusing the stop on it, one whose `review_rounds_above_ceiling` entry differs from its line refused by `review-guard.py` the same way before any snapshot, and a brief whose `merge_unit` differs from its `Merge unit:` line refused by both; a plan fixture shaped like the TDP's first increments with `historic: true`, in a temporary git repository the demonstration builds whose history holds the squash subjects merging the marked IDs, `--base` naming its branch (as the increments' tests do, since a scratch fixture has no merged set), giving `plan-check.py` no shape finding, and the same mark on an ID that history does not hold a finding; a retired second reading shown gone (a bold `Satisfies` line outside an increment counted by nothing, the agreement rule naming it); `tests/test_header_schemas.py` green against D-005 and the `pyyaml` cross-check green over every fixture header; the currency test green over the design's eleven enumerated retired readings, each matched as a use; the full suite green; `diagram-check.py --mode code` RESULT OK with no `TESTS` line.
Alters: the programs' reading of a record that carries a header (header first; the two readings of eleven fields become one) and, for a record without one, the one fallback reading `bridge_env` keeps, the retired second readings gone (a bold `Satisfies` line outside an increment, a forgiving `Workflows:` parse, an empty `Ceiling above twice:` value, `conformance-check.py`'s two readings of `Phase:`, `plan-check.py`'s column-position read of the index, `diagram-check.py`'s whole-text `R_RX` over the register, `plan-check.py`'s own `merged_ids`: the design's enumerated list of eleven); every checker gains the `HEADER MALFORMED` (exit 2) and `HEADER DISAGREES` (a finding) findings, each table's owning checker the `columns` and `roles.<role>` findings, the launcher and the guard a `header:` refusal covering a malformed or disagreeing brief or run-parameters header (each program comparing the keys and the lifts it reads with their lines before any governs a launch or a snapshot; `ceiling_above_twice` read as a list of lifts by kind), the evaluation a `header malformed` line, the hooks a `header disagrees: <key>` line, the loop guard's a refused stop; `inventory-check.py`'s caps count the body, its help text saying so and the command reference regenerated; `plan-check.py` exempts a `historic: true` increment merged on `main` from its shape rules; `bridge-run.py`'s plan-id read covers every `docs/BUILD_PLAN*.md`; everything else unchanged.

## The program release — the plan addendum (2026-11-13)

The program release's increments follow the standing constraints above as release S left them, and these of their own, from the design (docs/DESIGN.md, "Change: the program release") and rows R-066 to R-104. **The gate is release S's**: a numbered brief after the first carries its `Round cause:` line; a finding that sends work back names its class and its path; a records-only pull request takes the short gate; step 4 runs the increment's own test files, stripped, through the launcher, and the whole suite is CI's, once per pull request; a test that reads the pull-request variables is run alone with them set (KP-074). **The programs are the builder's, their acceptance tests the supervisor's and named from the start** (ISS-017), as release B2's were: written before the brief, committed before the checkpoint, named in the brief as fixed points, each red on the checkpoint by node id or an invariant the increment's mutants show strong; a test of a name the builder adds reaches it so that it collects on the checkpoint and fails there. **A program item's regression test fails on 2026.11.12a**, which the brief shows by the `--checkpoint` run, but for the two items the design found already right (the all-omitted index, the HARDENING column), whose tests hold what stands. **A fix of a program that reads untrusted input is briefed by every read** (KP-078; and where the input is a guard's own record, KP-077): TASK-059, TASK-060, TASK-062, TASK-063, TASK-065, TASK-066, TASK-067 and TASK-083 name, from the first brief, every form the changed reading must refuse and every refusal it must keep, and each carries a mutants file swept with a recorded operator set (a refusal removed, a comparison inverted, a form dropped from a list, the resolved path taken unresolved, a quote rule relaxed) before the stage review reads the stage (ISS-007, ISS-019); the security auditor reads each of those eight. **Instruction text is the supervisor's and has no wording test**: the sentences the program items need are gathered in two text increments, TASK-082 at the end of stage P2 and TASK-078 at the end of stage P3, each after the programs it names have merged, and a row with a program and a sentence reads built at its text increment, the later of its two (R-078, R-081, R-083, R-085, R-087 and R-105 at TASK-082; R-088, R-089, R-090, R-091, R-092, R-095 and R-097 at TASK-078), the program increment's merge leaving the row planned; its Evidence then names the test and the text. **The rule budget is not raised**: the rulebook stands at 149,999 of 150,000 words and the digest at 9,500 of 9,500 characters, so every text increment retires at least the words it adds, measured by `tests/test_release_s_budgets.py` and quoted in its accept commit, and TASK-082 and TASK-078 together leave the 200 words TASK-080 needs. **No passage is retired or moved before TASK-057 merges**; from then on every text increment (TASK-082, TASK-078, TASK-079, TASK-080, and the stamp) depends on TASK-057 and its pull request passes `trace-check.py --moves`, each retired or reworded passage excused by its own entry in `docs/TRACE_MOVES.md`, pasted from the mode's own failure line and read by a reviewer; such an increment's Scope names `bridge/cursor-bridge/workflow/trace-exceptions.md` beside that record, for the pinned mode's entries, and its accept commit lists the sentences it adds and the passages it retires with their word counts. **A new program** (TASK-067, TASK-068) is loaded by `tools/dump-cli.py`, so its increment carries the command reference regenerated, its allow entry in `settings.bridge.json` pinned by `tests/test_allowlist.py`, and D-002's `dump_cli` edge to it, the edge the supervisor's commit on the increment's branch; an increment that adds or changes an option of an existing program carries the reference regenerated and `tools/dump-cli.py --check` green. **`gh` is a stand-in in every test** of the rule's reader, `ready`, the watch's wait and the security step, on replies recorded once from this repository and committed under `tests/fixtures/github/`, each cut to the fields a program reads, its owner and repository replaced by placeholders; these are the contract captures of the trait External services, which the design proposes for the part and whose additions these increments carry whatever the gate says of the label: each capture is made live and attended by the supervisor, with the owner's `gh` login and by GET alone, before the brief of the first increment that replays it (TASK-064 the seven calls, TASK-083 and TASK-065 the states of a head, TASK-077 the workflow's state, its runs and the alerts), no test rests on a reply the builder invents (KP-020), a state this repository cannot be brought into being built from a captured reply by changing only the fields the fixture's header names, and every brief of those four increments names the time limit and the retry limit of each call it adds, which `diff-reviewer` reads in the diff; no test reads GitHub, another project's records or a per-computer file. **A host fact is observed before the text that relies on it** (the type's rule): TASK-079 and TASK-080 each open with their probe in Claude Code, recorded in HARDENING. Every pull request carries `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` as it reads at that pull request's merge (ISS-009). No increment runs in parallel: the heavy slot is one, and the launcher, the guard and `bridge_env.py` are shared by most. Each stage closes as release S's text has it: the refactoring pass, one whole pass of both reviewers over the stage's change, the demonstration live, the reflection items; **the stage review of the release's whole text runs before the stamp** (the type's rule at `planning.stages`), after TASK-080 and before TASK-081, and the demonstration of stage P4 runs on the stamped tree before the install (release S's rule in the type's text).
